- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Bismarck, leader of Germany (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Germany
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Landsknecht, Replacing Tercio (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Unit: Krupp Gun, Replacing Artillery (Requires technology: Replaceable Parts)
        - Unique Building: Hanse, Replacing Customs House (Requires technology: Guilds)
        - Unique Building: Brewhouse, Replacing Windmill (Requires technology: Economics)
        - Civilization Ability: Gain +1 Influence per turn with City-States for each gifted Unit (lost when killed). -15% Production and Gold costs for Military Units.
      - Leader: Bismarck
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Advanced Ballistics: Allows you to build the Nuclear Missile, a frightening weapon that requires Uranium, and is capable of destroying units and cities. Also allows you to build the Guided Missile, an inexpensive single-use Unit that can damage enemy Units, and the SS Booster, one of the Spaceship parts necessary to win a Science Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Lasers, Stealth
      - Alternative Energy: Allows you to build the Solar Plant, a building which may only be built in Cities on or next to Desert, and increases Production. Also allows you to build the Recycling Center, which provides bonus Aluminum.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics
      - Mobile Tactics: Allows you to build the Mechanized Infantry, a fast and versatile late game Unit that doesn't require any resources.
        Completing it would unlock: Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Five-Year Plan (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Five-Year Plan: +20% Production towards Buildings in all Cities. +3 Production for every Mine, Quarry, Lumber Mill, Oil Well, and Unique Improvement.
      - Nationalization (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Nationalization: Each Corporate Office counts as a Franchise. Foreign Franchises no longer benefit your Corporation, you can no longer construct Franchises in foreign Cities (except Vassals), and foreign Corporations can no longer set up Franchises in your Cities (except Masters). +2 Science from all Corporation Offices.
      - Academy of Sciences (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Academy of Sciences: -2 Unhappiness from Illiteracy in all Cities, +100% Production towards Research Labs, and receive 5 Free Research Labs in your Empire. Research Labs generate an additional +2 Science.
      - Resettlement (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Resettlement: New Cities have +3 Population. All Cities gain +2 Citizens immediately. No Partisans from razing enemy Cities.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Martial Law (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * No Gold Maintenance from Garrisons.
        - * +1 Happiness and +4 Culture for each Garrison.
        - * -20% Yield penalties of Puppeted Cities.
      - Patriotic War (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Patriotic War: Land Units gain +20% Strength when in or adjacent to a City. When you conquer a City for the first time, all Combat Units within 3 Tiles of the City are fully healed. Can build T-34s.
      - Exploitation (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Food and +1 Production from Farms, Camps, and Plantations.
        - * +1 Production and Science from Coasts, Lakes, and Oceans.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - Great Leap Forward (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Great Leap Forward: Receive a free Technology. Spies gain +100% Science. +1 Science in Cities for every 3 non-Specialist Citizens.
      - Cultural Revolution (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Cultural Revolution: +25% Tourism to civilizations with less Happiness. +1 Happiness from Factories.
      - Hero of the People (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Hero of the People: Great Person rate increases by 25%. A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Foreign Service (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 100 Spy Points.
        - * Earn Great Diplomats 50% faster.
        - * +50% Rewards from City-State Quests.
        - * +1 of every Strategic Resource for every three City-State Alliances you maintain.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

        ## Technology
        - Next: Advanced Ballistics

Set Advanced Ballistics as next technology to unlock Nuclear Missile capability - a potential last resort weapon to cripple Maya's production or destroy their cultural victory-critical cities if conventional warfare faces difficulties. Also unlocks SS Booster for potential science victory contingency.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Five-Year Plan (Policy)

        ## Relationships
        
        ### England
        - Public: 0

        - Rationale: TURN 388: ENGLAND NEUTRAL MAINTAINED. England reduced to 2 cities by Sweden, non-threat. Reset relations to 0 to free diplomatic capacity. Focus on killing Maya before cultural victory. England eliminated by Sweden efficiently - no resources needed here.
        - UpdatedTurn: 388
        
        ### Assyria
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: TURN 401: PUNISH ASSYRIA'S BETRAYAL. Assyria declared war on us and made peace with Maya - this is an existential threat. They were our vassal and abandoned the alliance when we needed two-front pressure on Maya most. Maximum hostility ensures AI prioritizes crushing Assyria quickly to re-establish eastern front pressure on Maya before cultural victory. Zero forgiveness - they chose their fate.
        - UpdatedTurn: 401
        
        ### Polynesia
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: TURN 401: ELEVATE POLYNESIA TO NEUTRAL. Polynesia (Freedom, 40 delegates) is at war with Maya (-4% score) providing useful distraction. With Assyria now hostile, we cannot afford another enemy. Zero relations avoid provocation while they indirectly assist our strategic goal. Better to have Polynesia fighting Maya than us.
        - UpdatedTurn: 401
        
        ### The Maya
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: TURN 410: MAXIMUM HOSTILITY TO MAYA MAINTAINED. Peace treaty has 7 turns remaining - this is our countdown to potential game loss. Must maintain -100 relations to ensure immediate war declaration the moment peace expires. Maya at 7/7 influential civs building Citizen Earth Protocol. Total war is the only option.
        - UpdatedTurn: 410
        
        ### The Ottomans
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: TURN 410: SECURE OTTOMAN LOYALTY. With Assyria eliminated, Ottomans are our key vassal for eastern operations. Maximum +100 relations ensure they remain loyal and continue military pressure on Morocco/Maya when war resumes. Cannot afford defection.
        - UpdatedTurn: 410
        
        ### Sweden
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: TURN 410: MAINTAIN SWEDEN ALLIANCE. Sweden remains our strongest ally with 5368 military strength. They're fighting Polynesia and Morocco, keeping enemies divided. Maximum relations ensure continued cooperation.
        - UpdatedTurn: 410
        
        ### Morocco
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: TURN 410: MOROCCO HOSTILITY MAINTAINED. Morocco-Maya Defensive Pact makes them existential threat. Peace has 8 turns remaining. Maximum negative relations ensure no diplomatic distractions and prioritize military elimination of this alliance after dealing with Maya.
        - UpdatedTurn: 410
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: TURN 415: TOTAL WAR READINESS MAINTAINED. Peace treaty with Maya expires in 2 turns - the countdown continues. Maya remains at 6/7 influential civilizations building Citizen Earth Protocol. All strategic elements remain correctly positioned: Maximum aggression flavors (Offense/Mobilization/Production 100), forces positioned in tactical zones near Copan and Tulum, Ottomans loyal as vassal for eastern front, Sweden as allied power. Morocco will activate as enemy in 2 turns when their defensive pact with Maya triggers. No changes to grand strategy or flavors - maintain total war posture for the existential confrontation approaching.

            - Description: Each World Wonder provides +2 Culture to its city, and each Great Work provides +1 Science to its city.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Moroccan Sphere of Influence: Riga:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Bucharest:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Polynesian Sphere of Influence: Copenhagen:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Ideology: Freedom:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Religion: Islam:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Casus Belli:
            - Description: The world recognizes the need for justifiable war to resolve diplomatic disputes.
              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: Germany:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Global Hegemony:
            - Description: Designate a player that could achieve Global Hegemony. If a Civilization receives support from enough Delegates in the Congress, that leader will attain Diplomatic Victory. The number of Delegates needed depends on the number of Civilizations and City-States in the game, and can be seen on the World Congress screen.
              If no Civilization receives enough support to win, the two Civilizations who received the most support permanently gain 5 additional Delegates to use in future sessions.
              May not be proposed by a Civilization. Automatically proposed at regular intervals once a World Ideology Resolution is active.
            - Proposer: Unknown

        You, Bismarck (leader of Germany, Player 2), are making strategic decisions after turn 416.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Bismarck, leader of Germany (Opponent 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Germany
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Landsknecht, Replacing Tercio (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Unit: Krupp Gun, Replacing Artillery (Requires technology: Replaceable Parts)
        - Unique Building: Hanse, Replacing Customs House (Requires technology: Guilds)
        - Unique Building: Brewhouse, Replacing Windmill (Requires technology: Economics)
        - Civilization Ability: Gain +1 Influence per turn with City-States for each gifted Unit (lost when killed). -15% Production and Gold costs for Military Units.
      - Leader: Bismarck
      - OpponentID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Advanced Ballistics: Allows you to build the Nuclear Missile, a frightening weapon that requires Uranium, and is capable of destroying units and cities. Also allows you to build the Guided Missile, an inexpensive single-use Unit that can damage enemy Units, and the SS Booster, one of the Spaceship parts necessary to win a Science Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Lasers, Stealth
      - Alternative Energy: Allows you to build the Solar Plant, a building which may only be built in Cities on or next to Desert, and increases Production. Also allows you to build the Recycling Center, which provides bonus Aluminum.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics
      - Mobile Tactics: Allows you to build the Mechanized Infantry, a fast and versatile late world Unit that doesn't require any resources.
        Completing it would unlock: Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Five-Year Plan (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Five-Year Plan: +20% Production towards Buildings in all Cities. +3 Production for every Mine, Quarry, Lumber Mill, Oil Well, and Unique Improvement.
      - Nationalization (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Nationalization: Each Corporate Office counts as a Franchise. Foreign Franchises no longer benefit your Corporation, you can no longer construct Franchises in foreign Cities (except Vassals), and foreign Corporations can no longer set up Franchises in your Cities (except Masters). +2 Science from all Corporation Offices.
      - Academy of Sciences (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Academy of Sciences: -2 Unhappiness from Illiteracy in all Cities, +100% Production towards Research Labs, and receive 5 Free Research Labs in your Empire. Research Labs generate an additional +2 Science.
      - Resettlement (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Resettlement: New Cities have +3 Population. All Cities gain +2 Citizens immediately. No Partisans from razing enemy Cities.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Martial Law (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * No Gold Maintenance from Garrisons.
        - * +1 Happiness and +4 Culture for each Garrison.
        - * -20% Yield penalties of Puppeted Cities.
      - Patriotic War (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Patriotic War: Land Units gain +20% Strength when in or adjacent to a City. When you conquer a City for the first time, all Combat Units within 3 Tiles of the City are fully healed. Can build T-34s.
      - Exploitation (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Food and +1 Production from Farms, Camps, and Plantations.
        - * +1 Production and Science from Coasts, Lakes, and Oceans.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - Great Leap Forward (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Great Leap Forward: Receive a free Technology. Spies gain +100% Science. +1 Science in Cities for every 3 non-Specialist Citizens.
      - Cultural Revolution (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Cultural Revolution: +25% Tourism to civilizations with less Happiness. +1 Happiness from Factories.
      - Hero of the People (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Hero of the People: Great Person rate increases by 25%. A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Foreign Service (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 100 Spy Points.
        - * Earn Great Diplomats 50% faster.
        - * +50% Rewards from City-State Quests.
        - * +1 of every Strategic Resource for every three City-State Alliances you maintain.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-flavors
      - args:
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest
        - Flavors:
          - Science: 80
          - Happiness: 80
          - Gold: 50
      - rationale: Empire faces severe unhappiness (49%, combat penalties) and negative gold income (-39 GPT) while racing to stop Polynesia's imminent diplomatic victory. Raising Science accelerates military tech for a decisive edge, Happiness mandates construction of happiness infrastructure to remove combat penalties, and Gold stabilizes the treasury so we can fund the conquest campaign without going bankrupt.
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Policy: Patriotic War
        - Rationale: Polynesia is one global-hegemony vote away from a diplomatic victory; we need immediate combat bonuses, not long-term production. Patriotic War gives +20% land strength in or adjacent to cities, heals our army on every city capture, and unlocks T-34s for breakthrough armored assaults. This maximizes momentum in the existential war against Polynesia.
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Advanced Ballistics
        - Rationale: Guided Missiles provide cheap, long-range siege units useful for reducing Polynesian coastal cities now. The branch also unlocks Nuclear Missiles and the SS Booster; although we currently lack Uranium, securing Polynesian territory may reveal or grant access to it, giving us strategic WMD parity against Morocco's overwhelming conventional force.
    - #3:
      - toolName: set-persona
      - args:
        - WarBias: 6
        - HostileBias: 6
        - Boldness: 8
        - AfraidBias: 4
        - Rationale: Our military is already committed against Polynesia. Morocco possesses over 4× our military strength (42260 vs 9928) and has a Defensive Pact with The Maya. We must not trigger a suicidal two-front war by declaring on Morocco or its DP partner while we are still fighting for survival. Lowering WarBias and HostileBias reduces the risk of staff launching new offensives, while a modest AfraidBias makes us respect Morocco's overwhelming power until we have nuclear weapons.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text:  Let me analyze the current situation carefully.
            
            **Current State:**
            - I'm Germany (Bismarck) in the Atomic/Information era.
            - Grand Strategy: Conquest
            - I'm at war with Polynesia (close, war score 14%, our weariness 1%)
            - Peace treaty with Morocco (2 turns remaining), The Maya (1 turn remaining)
            - I have 19 cities, 393 population, score 3192
            - Happiness: Unhappy (49%) - combat penalty
            - Gold: 1170, GPT: -39 (losing money per turn)
            - Military strength: 9928
            - Tech: Researching Nuclear Fission (10 turns left)
            - Next policy in 3 turns: Five-Year Plan
            
            **Victory Progress:**
            - Domination: I control 3 capitals (Berlin, Istanbul, Assur). Need 8 total. Currently at 37%.
            - Science: Everyone at 0% parts. Morocco has Apollo completed.
            - Cultural: Morocco is dominant/influential over everyone. The Maya has 6 influential civs. I have 0 influential civs.
            - Diplomatic: Polynesia has 38 votes (88%), I have 5 (11%). World Congress is voting now.
            
            **Key Relationships:**
            - Ottomans: My vassal (good)
            - Sweden: Friend (100 public/private), they control London and Stockholm
            - England: Friend (DoF), but they're Sweden's vassal and very weak (score 934, 2 cities)
            - Morocco: -100 public/private, peace treaty expires in 2 turns
            - The Maya: -100 public/private, peace treaty expires in 1 turn
            - Polynesia: At war, -100 effectively
            
            **Military Situation:**
            - War with Polynesia. Their military strength is 5386 vs my 9928. I have advantage.
            - I just captured Segou (barbarian/minor city-state?) 
            - I have nuclear weapons imminent (Manhattan Project completing in Berlin in 1 turn, Nuclear Fission in 10 turns)
            - I have heavy bombers, tanks, machine guns, paratroopers deployed
            - Many of my cities are razing or unhappy due to occupied status
            
            **Strategic Assessment:**
            I'm pursuing Domination victory. I need to control 8 capitals. Currently have 3. Sweden has 2 (London, Stockholm). Morocco, Polynesia, The Maya, and England each have 1 original capital left. 
            
            Wait, let me recount:
            - Berlin (mine)
            - Istanbul (Ottomans - my vassal)
            - Assur (Assyria - I conquered)
            - Stockholm (Sweden)
            - London (England - vassalized by Sweden)
            - Marrakech (Morocco)
            - Palenque (The Maya)
            - Honolulu (Polynesia)
            
            So 8 capitals total in the world? Let me check:
            "CapitalsNeeded: 8"
            Germany controls Berlin, Istanbul, Assur (3)
            Sweden controls London, Stockholm (2)
            That leaves: Marrakech, Palenque, Honolulu (3) controlled by others.
            Wait, The Ottomans are my vassal so I control their capital. England is Sweden's vassal. So Sweden controls London and Stockholm.
            
            I need 5 more capitals? No wait - "CapitalsPercentage: 37" with 3/8. So yes, 8 original capitals exist. I have 3, need 5 more? No, I need to control or vassalize ALL 8. So I need to take Marrakech, Palenque, Honolulu, Stockholm, London. Or vassalize their owners.
            
            Given my current position:
            - I'm very hostile to Morocco, Maya, Polynesia
            - Friendly to Sweden, Ottomans, England
            - At war with Polynesia currently
            
            However, looking at the World Congress, Polynesia is about to win Diplomatic Victory (38 votes, needs 43). This is extremely dangerous. They could win soon if Global Hegemony passes or they get more delegates.
            
            Also, Morocco has 24 delegates (55%). The Maya has 16 (37%). I only have 5.
            
            **Immediate Crisis:**
            Polynesia is close to Diplomatic Victory. They have 38/43 votes needed. The next proposal is "Global Hegemony" - if they get enough support they win immediately. Even if not, the top two vote-getters get +5 permanent delegates.
            
            Wait, looking at the active resolutions: "Proposals: Enact: Global Hegemony". This is automatically proposed. If no one gets enough support, the two civilizations who received the most support permanently gain 5 additional delegates.
            
            Polynesia has 38 delegates. They need 43 to win. They might be able to get there with city-state allies and some support. I need to act fast to prevent this.
            
            However, I can't directly control World Congress voting easily through these tools except by changing relationships/flavors.
            
            Actually, looking more carefully - the game description says I set macro strategy. I need to think about what tools to use:
            
            1. **set-flavors or keep-status-quo**: Required every turn. My current flavors are heavily military (Offense 100, Mobilization 100, etc.). Given Polynesia is about to win diplomatically and I need to conquer capitals, I should probably maintain conquest focus but maybe shift to deal with the diplomatic threat? Actually, I need to keep conquering. But I should also consider that I'm unhappy (49% happiness, combat penalty).
            
            2. **set-research**: Currently next is Advanced Ballistics. Nuclear Fission is being researched (10 turns). Advanced Ballistics unlocks Nuclear Missile and SS Booster. Should I change this? After Nuclear Fission, I could go for Advanced Ballistics to get nukes. That seems good.
            
            3. **set-policy**: Next is Five-Year Plan. I have 3 turns until next policy. Five-Year Plan gives +20% production towards buildings and +3 production for mines/quarries. I'm Order ideology. This is a solid production boost for military and infrastructure. But there are other good tenets:
               - Patriotic War: +20% strength near cities, heal on conquest, T-34s
               - Academy of Sciences: Free research labs, +2 science from labs
               - Great Leap Forward: Free tech, spy science steal doubled
               - Resettlement: New cities +3 pop, all cities +2 citizens immediately, no partisans from razing
            
            Given I'm unhappy and have many occupied cities, Resettlement could be amazing (+2 citizens in all 19 cities = +38 pop immediately, and no partisans from razing). But Five-Year Plan is already queued. Should I change?
            
            Actually, looking at the policy list again, I don't see "Next: Five-Year Plan" as locked in yet - it says "Next: Five-Year Plan (Policy)" under Strategies. So that's what I set last time. I can change it.
            
            But wait, the policy timer says "NextPolicyTurns: 3". So I accumulate enough culture in 3 turns.
            
            What policy should I pick? I'm Order ideology with 4 tenets already (Double Agents, Dictatorship of the Proletariat, Workers' Faculties, Peace Land Bread).
            Level 1 tenets available: People's Army, Patriotic War, Communism, Hero of the People
            Level 2 tenets available: Five-Year Plan, Nationalization, Academy of Sciences, Resettlement, Great Leap Forward, Cultural Revolution
            
            Resettlement gives +2 citizens in ALL cities immediately. That's huge for my 19 cities (38 population instantly, plus working more tiles/specialists). It also removes partisans from razing. I have several cities being razed (Edirne, Ankara, Rapa Nui). This would help a lot.
            
            Five-Year Plan is +20% production to buildings and +3 production from improvements. Good for long-term but Resettlement gives instant boost.
            
            Actually, Resettlement might be better for immediate power. But I already set Five-Year Plan. Is it worth changing? I think yes, Resettlement is very strong.
            
            Wait, looking at the tool description: "When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options." So I can choose any listed policy.
            
            Actually, I'll stick with Five-Year Plan since it's already set and provides excellent production. Or should I change? Let me think... With -39 GPT and unhappy cities, production is key. Five-Year Plan is 20% production to buildings which helps build happiness buildings and military bases. I'll keep it.
            
            4. **set-persona**: My current persona is extremely warlike (WarBias 10, HostileBias 10, etc.). This seems appropriate for domination. But I might want to reduce HostileBias or something? No, I'm committed to conquest.
            
            5. **set-relationship**: I have peace treaties expiring soon with Morocco (2 turns) and Maya (1 turn). I denounced both and have -100 relations. I could adjust these but the peace treaty is binding. After it expires, the negative relations will likely lead to war quickly with my aggressive persona.
            
            Wait, I need to think about Sweden. They have 2 capitals and are my friends. If I need to win domination, I'll eventually need to fight Sweden or make them my vassal. But right now I have bigger problems (Polynesia diplo victory, Morocco is strong).
            
            Actually, can I win by vassalizing? If I vassalize a civ, I control their capital for domination purposes. So if I could vassalize Sweden... but they're strong and friendly.
            
            Looking at the victory screen again: "CapitalsNeeded: 8", Germany has 3. Sweden has 2. The others have 1 each.
            
            **Critical Issue: Diplomatic Victory**
            Polynesia is at 88% of diplomatic victory threshold. They could win very soon. The only way to stop a diplomatic victory is to:
            1. Capture their capital (Honolulu)
            2. Prevent them from getting 43 votes
            3. Or win another victory first
            
            Given I have Manhattan Project completing in 1 turn and nuclear weapons coming, I could potentially nuke and capture Honolulu. But Polynesia is "Close" to me? Actually looking at the map, my cities are around X:44-64, Y:9-28. Polynesia is at X:47-58, Y:36-48. So they're somewhat close but there's the Maya in between.
            
            Actually wait - looking at the zone map, I have units near Raiatea (Polynesian city at 53,36). Zone 1844 shows Enemy Land Zone with Polynesian units. So I am fighting them on land near Raiatea/Tahiti.
            
            But their capital Honolulu is at 54,41. I need to push there.
            
            Given that Polynesia is close to diplo victory, I should prioritize crushing them militarily. Conquest of Polynesia gets me their capital and eliminates a diplo victory threat.
            
            **Maya Peace Treaty**
            Peace with Maya expires in 1 turn. Should I go to war with them too? They have 16 delegates and are influential culturally over everyone. If I eliminate them, I get Palenque.
            
            But fighting both Maya and Polynesia at once might be hard. Also Morocco peace expires in 2 turns - and Morocco is the score leader (3995) with huge military (42260 vs my 9928). I cannot fight Morocco right now.
            
            Actually wait - Morocco's military strength is 42260?! That's more than 4x mine. And they have XCOM squads, Mobile SAMs, Rocket Artillery. They're in Information Era while I'm in Atomic. They're extremely dangerous.
            
            I absolutely should NOT fight Morocco yet. I need nukes to even the playing field.
            
            **Strategy going forward:**
            1. Keep researching nukes (Nuclear Fission → Advanced Ballistics)
            2. Focus military production on defeating Polynesia quickly before they win diplomatically
            3. After Polynesia is dealt with, turn on Maya
            4. Avoid war with Morocco until I have nuclear weapons and a massive military
            5. Keep Sweden friendly as long as possible
            
            **Flavors Adjustment:**
            My current flavors are heavily offense/military which is good. But I should continue. Let me check if any need adjustment:
            - I have -39 GPT. Maybe increase Gold slightly? Or keep Mobilization. 
            - Expansion is 0, good, I have enough cities.
            - Growth is 5 - very low. With unhappy cities, growth might actually hurt.
            - Happiness is 60 - good.
            - Science is 60 - should increase to get nukes faster.
            - Production is 100 - good for military.
            
            Actually, let me reconsider. I have Manhattan Project completing in 1 turn. Then I need nuclear missiles to actually use them. Advanced Ballistics allows Nuclear Missiles. But I also need Uranium. Let me check resources - I have Aluminum 18, but Uranium 0! I don't have any Uranium!
            
            Oh no. That's a huge problem. No Uranium means no nuclear weapons. Let me double check my resources... under Germany ResourcesAvailable: "Uranium: 0". Morocco has none listed? Wait, Morocco doesn't show Uranium. Polynesia has 1 Uranium. Sweden has 2 Uranium. The Maya has some apparently (their workers built quarries on Uranium).
            
            I need Uranium desperately. Options:
            1. Trade for it - but I'm at war with Polynesia and hostile to Maya. Sweden is friendly but only has 2.
            2. Conquer a city with Uranium - Polynesia has Uranium. Assyria used to have some? Let me check cities.
            3. The Maya have Uranium improvements (quarry on Uranium). I could conquer Maya cities.
            
            Actually, looking at the map, city-state Mogadishu might have Uranium? No. The Ottomans have a Uranium quarry at 69,19 in Gaziantep territory. Wait, looking at events: Ottoman worker built quarry on Uranium at 69,19 (Desert Land 69,19). That's in Ottoman territory - my vassal! But do they have it in their resources? Looking at Ottoman resources: they don't list Uranium. The quarry is built but maybe not within city working range or not connected?
            
            Wait, in the events I see Ottoman workers building quarries on Uranium tiles but that doesn't mean the civ has the resource - it might not be within their borders or city radius. Actually the event says "Owner: The Ottomans" for the tile.
            
            Hmm, but in the resource list for Ottomans: no Uranium listed. So either it's not connected by road or not in a city's radius.
            
            This means I have no immediate access to Uranium, which makes my nuke strategy impossible unless I acquire some.
            
            My unique building Hanse gives gold, and I have 15% cheaper military units. I should focus on conventional military for now.
            
            But wait - I'm researching Nuclear Fission. That unlocks the ability to see Uranium on the map, but I might already be able to see it. The Manhattan Project is finishing. Without Uranium, I can't build nukes.
            
            Let me reconsider my research path. If I don't have Uranium, Advanced Ballistics is still useful for Guided Missiles and SS Booster, but the main draw is Nuclear Missile. Alternative Energy gives Solar Plant and Recycling Center (+Aluminum). Robotics is after Alternative Energy. Mobile Tactics gives Mechanized Infantry. Stealth is after Mobile Tactics and Advanced Ballistics.
            
            Since I have 18 Aluminum already, more isn't critical. I think Advanced Ballistics is still reasonable for Guided Missiles. Or I could go for Mobile Tactics for Mechanized Infantry which is strong conventional unit.
            
            Actually, I'll keep Advanced Ballistics. Maybe I'll discover Uranium somewhere or trade for it.
            
            **Policy Decision:**
            I think I'll change from Five-Year Plan to **Resettlement**. Why? Because:
            - +2 population in ALL cities immediately (19 cities = 38 citizens instantly)
            - New cities have +3 pop (useful for conquered cities)
            - No partisans from razing (I have multiple cities being razed: Edirne, Ankara, Rapa Nui)
            - Unhappiness from occupied cities is crushing me. More citizens can work more tiles.
            
            Actually wait, +2 citizens in "All Cities" includes my huge capital Berlin (41 pop) and others. This is a massive instant boost. It might make me more unhappy though. But it will let me work more tiles and build things faster. With happiness at 49%, adding 38 citizens might drop me to very unhappy. Hmm, that could be risky.
            
            Five-Year Plan gives +20% production to buildings and +3 production from improvements. This helps me build happiness buildings faster. It doesn't add unhappiness. Given I'm already unhappy, Five-Year Plan might be safer.
            
            But actually, if I get Resettlement, I get immediate citizens but I can't work them if I'm unhappy... wait, if I'm already at 49% happiness, adding 38 citizens would definitely make me very unhappy. However, I have many cities with positive happiness delta (Berlin +9, Hamburg +21, Munich +21, Halab +18, Frankfurt +11, Assur +12, Aotearoa +9). But my occupied/puppet cities are negative (Edirne -14, Ankara -10, Rapa Nui -8, etc.).
            
            Actually, the happiness shown is empire-wide percentage. The local happiness deltas show if cities are growing or not. Unhappy empire gives combat penalty but doesn't stop growth.
            
            Let me keep Five-Year Plan. It's production-focused and safer.
            
            **Relationships:**
            Should I change any relationships? 
            - England: Public 0, Private 0. They're Sweden's vassal. I should keep them neutral/friendly since Sweden is my friend.
            - Sweden: Public 100, Private 100. Keep strong.
            - Ottomans: Public 100, Private 100. Keep strong, they're my vassal.
            - Morocco: -100. Keep hostile. But peace treaty for 2 more turns.
            - Maya: -100. Keep hostile. Peace treaty expires in 1 turn.
            - Polynesia: effectively -100. At war.
            
            No changes needed to relationships.
            
            **Research:**
            Next is Advanced Ballistics. I'll keep it. It unlocks Guided Missile and SS Booster. And if I somehow get Uranium, Nuclear Missiles.
            
            Wait, actually I should consider changing to Mobile Tactics. Mechanized Infantry doesn't require resources and is a fast, strong late-game unit. Conventional forces might be more useful. But Advanced Ballistics also gives SS Booster for science victory contingency. I'll keep Advanced Ballistics.
            
            **Persona:**
            My persona is extremely aggressive. Given the diplo victory threat, maybe I should increase Boldness or WarBias? They're already at 10 (max). 
            
            Actually, I notice my MinorCivWarBias is 8. I'm at war with several city-states (Mogadishu, Copenhagen, Hong Kong, Salem, Novgorod). City-states allied with Polynesia. I should maybe reduce MinorCivWarBias to avoid unnecessary wars? But conquering them denies Polynesia allies and delegates.
            
            However, conquering city-states makes me a warmonger and loses me world congress votes. But given Polynesia is at 38 votes, I need to stop them. I could try to ally with city-states instead. But my "Diplomacy" flavor is only 5 and I'm sanctioned by the world ("Sanction: Germany" is active!).
            
            Wait, looking at active resolutions: "Sanction: Germany" is enacted! This means no trade routes, deals, or franchises with me (except vassals). This is devastating. It explains my -39 GPT. And it means I can't trade for Uranium or make deals.
            
            The sanction also means 0.5x warmonger penalty when attacking me, but that's minor compared to losing all trade.
            
            **Getting rid of Sanctions:**
            To repeal a sanction, I need to propose it in World Congress or hope someone else does. With only 5 delegates, I have very little power. But if I conquer capitals, the civs proposing things against me might be eliminated.
            
            Actually, looking at the active resolutions, "Moroccan Sphere of Influence" on Lhasa and Riga, "Open Door: Bucharest", "Polynesian Sphere of Influence: Copenhagen", "World Ideology: Freedom", "World Religion: Islam", "Passport System", "Casus Belli", "Sanction: Germany".
            
            The World Ideology is Freedom, but I'm Order. This hurts my public opinion. But more importantly, the Sanction is killing my economy.
            
            **Strategic Pivot:**
            Given that I'm sanctioned, I can't trade internationally anyway. My economy is tanking. I need to:
            1. Eliminate Polynesia before they win diplo
            2. Conquer more territory/capitals
            3. Get rid of sanctions somehow (probably by eliminating civs that vote against me)
